    - Pawns at a7, b7, c6, g7, h3.

    Evaluation:
    The White Queen is on f7. The Black King is on d8.
    If White plays Qf7d7, is it checkmate?
    - Qd7+
    - Black King is at d8.
    - Squares for King: c8, e8.
    - If Qd7, the King cannot stay at d8.
    - can the king move to e8? No, Queen at d7 covers e8.
    - can the king move to c8? No, Queen at d7 covers c8.
    - can the king take d7? No, maybe. Let's check if d7 is protected.
    - The Bishop at g4 covers d7! (g4 -> h5 -> f6 -> e7 -> d8? No. g4 -> f5 -> e6 -> d7).
    - Yes, Bg4 protects d7.
    - So, f7d7 is Checkmate!

    Wait, let me double check the bishop move g4d7.
    g4 to d7: g4 -> f5 -> e6 -> d7. That's a diagonal move.
    Let's check:
    g4: (7, 5) in some coordinate system.
    d7: (4, 2) in some coordinate system. (Using 1-8 for files a-h and 1-8 for ranks).
    g4 is (7, 4).
    d7 is (4, 7).
    Difference in file: 7-4 = 3.
    Difference in rank: 7-4 = 3.
    Since 3=3, it's a diagonal. So the Bishop at g4 does indeed protect d7.

    Is there any other piece that can take d7?
    Black pieces: Ra8, Kd8, Pa7, Pb7, Pc6, Pg7, Ph3.
    - Ra8 cannot take d7.
    - Pa7 cannot take d7.
    - Pb7 cannot take d7.
    - Pc6 cannot take d7.
    - Pg7 cannot take d7.

    - If Queen is at d7:
        - e8: Queen at d7 covers e8.
        - c8: Queen at d7 covers c8.
        - d8: Queen at d7 covers d8.
    - Thus, Qd7 is checkmate.

    Black: Ra8, Kd8, Pa7, Pb7, Pc6, Pg7, Ph3.
